       force_pass=True and choices to match the desired sequence:

          p_time = Parameter(name="time", value="10",
                                            choices=["10", "10", "30"], dtype=str,
                                            force_pass=True)

          p_temp = Parameter(name="temperature", value="300",
                                            choices=["300", "400", "300"], dtype=str,
                                            force_pass=True)

       This passes each pair of choices together in index order.

       Parameters are passed to the main machine-learning object during its initialization. At data
       generation, the non-force_pass parameters are sampled using a DoE method (Box-
       Behnken or random sampling, for example), while each of the generated combinations of
       parameter values takes all three time-temperature condition splits together as simulation
       inputs. See Using generate_data_ml With a Specific DoE on page 67 for more details on
       how force_pass impacts data generation.

       Please see Parameter on page 257 for more details.

Targets

Targets, the output of the simulation, can be scalar values or a set of values representing XY
curves. When using machine learning models, targets are defined by:

 target_obj = Target(name=<s>, values=<t>, dimension=<i>,
                                      [conditions=<d>,] [simulation_variables=<t>,]
                                      [reference_variables=<t>,] [error_method=<s>,]
                                      [range=<t>,] [abs_value_range=<t>,]
                                      [x_range=<t>,] [y_range=<t>,] [weight=<f>,]
                                      [tolerance=<f>,] [penalty=<o>,] [id=<s>,]
                                      [dtype=<k>])

Most of these arguments are the same as those in the scwcal module (seeAttributes for
Targets on page 31). The following arguments are unique to the mlcal module:

· dimension must be set to 0 for scalar targets and to 2 for XY curve targets.

· tolerance is used by adaptive reverse search. Solutions in which all targets are within
    tolerance are preferred to other solutions.

See Target on page 288 for more details.

Function Targets

Definition of function targets is same as the description in Function Targets on page 51 in
Chapter 2. However, in mlcal, only reverse_search_ml and subsequent

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  51

W-2024.09
Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module
Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

       generate_data_ml support the function targets. Other tasks, such as create_ml,
       train_ml and predict_ml ignore the function targets if provided and display a warning.

Supported Special Characters

When defining the names of parameters and targets, the special characters "-" and "_" are
allowed. Other special characters are not supported.

Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

       The core machine learning module of Sentaurus Calibration Workbench is mlcal. While
       high-level ML tasks exist in all Sentaurus Calibration Workbench modules, granular control
       of the machine-learning models and their use can only be found in mlcal. The mlcal
       module extends the basic Sentaurus Calibration Workbench module scwcal. That is, it
       contains all the tasks included in scwcal, plus additional ones unique to mlcal.

       Because it is intended to allow fine control in terms of machine learning model creation and
       setup, the intended user of mlcal is one who has at least some familiarity with machine
       learning and machine learning models.

Modules to Be Loaded

The following modules should be loaded when using mlcal:

from       scw.core.parameter import Parameter
from       scw.core.target import Target
from       scw.apps.mlcal import MLCal
from       scw.core.model import Model
from       scw.core.scwglobal import *

An instance of an mlcal application can be created with:

mlcal_obj = MLCal()

The MLCal object has the same options as SCWCal object of the scwcal module for many
items such as parent project, child project directory, queue, and so on. For more information,
see Setting Options for Calibrations on page 33 and set_option on page 284.

Setting up the Models

In Sentaurus Calibration Workbench, a single Model object maps input parameters to
targets. These come in two possible configurations: many-parameters-to-many-targets
(MxN) or many-parameters-to-one-target (MX1). In the former (MxN), a single specific

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                                   52

W-2024.09
Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module
Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

machine learning model is used to predict a group of targets based on a set of input
parameters. In the latter (Mx1), each target has its own machine learning model associated
with it and this model predicts this target value based on the inputs. In the Mx1 configuration,
this entire collection of ML models is a single Model object in Sentaurus Calibration
Workbench.

Note: It can sometimes be confusing as to which "model" is being referred to, the
    Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Model object or the machine learning model for a
    specific target. In general, mlcal functions take the Model object (or list of Model objects)
    as an input.

Figure 1   Sentaurus Calibration Workbench machine-learning model configurations

Instantiating a new Model object for a given target and the hyperparameters associated with
the contained machine learning model(s) is relatively simple:

 ml_0 = Model(name="ml_0", hyperparameters=hyperparameters)

where "hyperparameters" is a dictionary containing all the model details. The setup of this
dictionary is covered in the following section. The configuration is set with the framework
argument. An example of setting the framework to MxN is:

 ml_0 = Model(name="ml_0", hyperparameters=hyperparameters,
                         framework=MxN)

By default, framework=Mx1.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                      53

W-2024.09
Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module
Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

      Model Hyperparameters

       Hyperparameters are the parameters that define the specifics of the machine learning
       models (as opposed to the input "parameters"). They must be declared as a dictionary:

          hyperparameters = dict()

       For an Mx1 configuration, hyperparameters must be defined for each target, for example:

          hyperparameters["Y0"] = {"model_type": MLPR, ... }
          hyperparameters["Y1"] = {"model_type": ETR, ... }

       For an MxN configuration, hyperparameters are defined for a single model that predicts all
       targets:

          hyperparameters["All"] = {"model_type": MLPR, ... }

       All models have the following possible hyperparameters:
       · model_type (keyword): Specifies the machine learning model type
       · model_hyperparameters (dict): Specifies the hyperparameters for the machine

            learning model selected in model_type. These hyperparameters are unique to each
            model.
       · parameter_scaler (keyword): Specifies the scaling to apply to input parameters
       · target_scaler (keyword): Specifies the scaling to apply to output targets
       · feature_interaction_type (keyword): Specifies the type of feature interaction terms
            to be considered
       · feature_interaction_params (dict): Specifies the feature interaction settings
       · feature_selection_type (keyword): Specifies the type of feature selection algorithm
            to use
       · feature_selection_parameters (dict): Specifies the feature selection settings

       · retrain (bool): Specifies whether to retrain the model every time train_ml is called
       · encoder_type (keyword): Specifies the type of encoder to use for profile targets
       · encoder_params (dict): Specifies the profile encoder settings
       · optimizer_type (keyword): Specifies the optimizer to use when optimizing the

             model_hyperparameters

       · optimizer_params (dict): Specifies the hyperparameter optimizer settings
       Each of these settings is covered in more detail in later sections. Most of these are required
       only if the use of that particular feature is needed. For example, if feature selection is not
       desired, then the feature selection entries can be omitted from the dictionary.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  54

W-2024.09
Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module
Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

Model Type and Hyperparameters

A variety of different machine learning models have been included in Sentaurus Calibration
Workbench and can be specified by keyword. For example, if you want to specify the
multilayer perceptron regressor model for target Y0, then the hyperparameter setting is:

 hyperparameters["Y0"] = {"model_type": MLPR, ... }

To specify the hyperparameters of the model, the model_hyperparameter dictionary must
be set:

 hyperparameters["Y0"] = {"model_type": MLPR,
                                               "model_hyperparameters":

                                             {"hidden_layer_sizes": [64, 64, 64],
                                               ...}, ...}

The keywords and associated models can be found in the following table. Most of these
models can be found in the scikit-learn library [1]. As of right now, only regression models
can be used. The model_hyperparameters vary significantly between model types. Please
check the associated reference in the table to look-up the hyperparameters associated with
each model.

Table 5    Hyperparameter model types

       The information about the possible hyperparameters for ANNR is given in the following list:
       · activation_function: Activation function for hidden layers (Default "relu")
       · activation_func_args: Arguments for activation functions.
       · batch_size: Batch size (Default 100)
       · batch_normalization: Batch normalization function
       · batch_norm_args: Arguments for batch normalization
       · dropout: Dropout for hidden layers (Default 0.0)
       · hidden_layer_sizes: Size of hidden layers. (Default (16, 16))
       · kept_model: The model to be kept. "best_validated" means the model which has the

            best validation score. "best_trained" means the model which has the best training
            score. "last_trained" means the model at the end of the training iteration. (Default
            "best_validated")
       · learning_rate: Initial learning rate (Default 1e-3)
       · loss_function: Loss function (Default "mse")
       · loss_func_args: Arguments for loss_function.
       · model_filename: Model file name.
       · model: Model name to be loaded
       · monotonic_layers: List of bool to apply monotonic layers. For the first layer, instead of
            a single bool, a list of bool is available to control components individually. Number of
            layers should be number of hidden layers + 1. For example, [[False, True, True], True,
            True]
       · num_epochs: Number of epoch (Default 100)
       · num_threads: Number of threads
       · num_workers: Number of subprocesses to use for data loading. 0 means that the data is
            loaded in the main process. (Default 0)
       · optimizer: Optimizer (Default "adam")
       · opt_args: Arguments for optimizer
       · patience: Patience for early stop
       · random_state: Random state to split validation set (Default 42)
       · report_filename: Report file name

Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module

· report_interval: Report interval of epoch (Default 100)
· set_flush_denormal: Denormal floating numbers on CPU. If True, 1e-323 become 0.

    If False, 1e-323 become 1e-323. (Default True)
· validation_fraction: Validation fraction (Default 0.1)
· verbose_level: Verbose level (Default 1)
· warm_start: If True, warm starting (transfer learning) is applied. (Default False)
· weight_decay: Weight decay (L2 regularization) (Default 1e-4)
· weight_initialization: Weight initialization function
· weight_init_args: Arguments for weight_initialization.

Parameter and Target Scaling

To improve training speed and quality of a machine learning model, it is usually better to
scale the parameters or targets to values closer to unity. The scaling methods available in
Sentaurus Calibration Workbench are shown in Table 6. The default value of this
hyperparameter is Standard.

Table 6    Supported parameter and target scalers

Method     Description

Identity   No scaling applied

Standard   Standard scaler

MinMax     Min-max scaler

Quantile   Quantile transformer

Robust     Robust scaler

As an example, standard scaling of the input parameters is set in the hyperparameters by:

 hyperparameters["Y0"] = {..., "parameter_scaler": Standard, ...}

Feature Interaction

Feature interaction provides a method of increasing input parameters by including the
interaction terms between different input parameters. Currently, only polynomial features[10]
are supported. They can be added to the model hyperparameters using:

 hyperparameters["Y0"] = {..., "feature_interaction_type":
                                               PolynomialFeatures,
                                               "feature_interaction_params": {...},

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                        57

W-2024.09
Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module
Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

                                               ...}

where "feature_interaction_params" is a dictionary that specifies the settings for the
feature interaction. The possible dictionary terms are the following:

· degree: Specifies the degree for the polynomial feature interaction (default: 2).

· create_only_interaction: Specifies whether interaction features are generated and
    terms with the power of degree of the same input feature are excluded. If set to True,
    then only interaction features are generated and terms with the power of degree of the
    same input feature are excluded (default: False).

· include_bias: Specifies whether to generate bias terms as new features (default:
    False).

· scale: Specifies the scale for new polynomial features (default: Linear).

· save_new_features: Specifies whether to save a new data frame for input parameters
    as a CSV file (default: False).

· csv_filename: Sets the file name for saving the data set as a CSV file (default:
    "polynomialfeatures_dataset").

· check_correlations: Specifies whether to analyze the correlation between parameters
    using a heat map (default: False).

· correlation_image_filename: Sets the file name for saving an image of feature
    correlations as a PNG file (default: "feature_correlations").

· outfile_prefix: Sets the prefix of the output CSV file and image file (default:
    "n@node@_").

For example, you can generate second-order polynomial terms from the initial input
parameters without bias terms and save the result as a CSV file and PNG file:

 hyperparameters["Y0"] = {..., "feature_interaction_type":
                                               PolynomialFeatures,
                                               "feature_interaction_params":
                                               {"degree": 2,
                                               "create_only_interaction": False,
                                               "include_bias": False,
                                               "save_new_features": True},
                                               ...}

Feature Selection

Feature selection, which operates independently or in combination with feature interaction,
is a function that selects only features with higher importance by comparing the importance
of numerous parameters in various ways. Depending on the statistical method, the results
of selectively applying only a portion of the total parameters to the modeling can show higher
or similar performance compared to those using all parameters. Increasing the number of

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                        58

W-2024.09
Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module
Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

       parameters does not always guarantee performance improvement, and it can save time
       when modeling with fewer parameters. Feature selection can be defined in the model
       hyperparameters:

          hyperparameters["Y0"] = {..., "feature_selection_type": SHAP,
                                                       "feature_selection_parameters": {...}, ...}

       where feature_selection_parameters is a dictionary that specifies the settings for the
       feature interaction. The possible values for feature_selection_type are:

       · FI: Simple selection based on feature importance

       · SFS: Sequential feature selection along the "forward" or "backward" direction [11]

       · RFE: Recursive feature elimination [12].

       · SHAP: Shapley additive explanations [13].

       SFS and RFE work only for models that have the attribute coef_ or
       feature_importances_. Refer to the documentation for the specific model to determine
       this.

       You can set the model to perform recursive feature elimination using the full data set on all
       targets with a 10-fold cross-validation with the hyperparameter setting:

          hyperparameters["Y0"] = {..., "feature_selection_type": RFE,
                                                       "feature_selection_parameters":
                                                       {"data_set": Full,
                                                       "target": All,
                                                       "cv": 10}, ...}

       Alternatively, you can use a sequential feature selection method for all data and a target
       named "tar_1" with the "forward" direction, by generating five cross-validation sets with a
       15% test size:

          hyperparameters["Y0"] = {..., "feature_selection_type": SFS,
                                                       "feature_selection_parameters":
                                                       {"data_set": Full,
                                                       "target": "tar_1",
                                                       "cv": 5,
                                                       "test_size": 0.15,
                                                       "sfs_direction": "forward"}, ...}

       SHAP, a newly added option, is used as a feature selection by calculating statistical
       marginal contributions of features based on cooperative game theory:

          hyperparameters['Y0'] = {..., "feature_selection_type": SHAP,
                                                       "feature_selection_parameters":
                                                       {"data_set": Full,
                                                       "target": All,
                                                       "cv": 10,
                                                       "threshold": 0.01,
                                                       "shap_summary_type": "shap_summary",

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  59

W-2024.09
Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module
Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

                                                       "save_summary_figure": True},...}

       The possible feature_selection_parameters dictionary terms are:

       · data_set: Specifies the subset of data on which the model is trained. Options are Full,
            Passed, PassedTrimmed, and Trimmed (default: Full)

       · target: Specifies the target to select features by importance (default: All)

       · cv: Sets the number of cross-validation sets generated by the basic cross-validator
            ShuffleSplit (default: 10)

       · test_size: Sets the fraction between the test set and the total training set for
            cross-validation (default: 0.1)

       · scoring: Sets the score indicator to evaluate feature importance (default: "r2")

       · verbose: Sets the verbosity level (default: 1)

       · sfs_direction: Specifies the optimization direction for sequential feature selection.
            This argument works only for method=SFS. Options are "backward" (default) and

             "forward"

       · threshold: Sets the threshold with which to select valid features (default: 0.01)

       · random_seed: Sets the random seed for cross-validation (default: 1)

       · val_fraction: Sets the fraction between the validation set and the total dataset
            (default: 0.1)

       · retrain: Specifies whether to allow redefinition of an already trained model (default:
            False)

       · shap_summary_type: Sets the type of the shap summary plot. The available options are
            "shap_summary", "shap_beeswarm", and "heatmap". Used only for method=SHAP
            (default: "shap_summary").

       · save_summary_figure: Specifies whether to save the figure of feature selection
            summary. Used only for method=SHAP (default: True).

      Hyperparameter Optimization

       To increase model accuracy, the model hyperparameters often need to be optimized.
       Sentaurus Calibration Workbench includes two methods with which to accomplish this
       optimization: grid search and Bayesian search. A grid search tests every specified
       combination of hyperparameters, while a Bayesian search performs a search within the
       maximum and minimum bounds of the hyperparameters. In both cases, n-fold
       cross-validation is used with the training set data to determine the best set of
       hyperparameters. This optimization occurs during the training of the model.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  60

W-2024.09
Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module
Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

       To use grid search, first the model hyperparameter values must be declared as lists. Then
       the grid search settings can be specified. For example:

          hyperparameters = dict()
          hyperparameters["Y0"] = {"model_type": MLPR,

                                                       "model_hyperparameters":
                                                       {"hidden_layer_sizes":
                                                       [[64, 64, 64], [128,128,128]],
                                                       "alpha": [0.1, 0.3, 1.0],
                                                       "max_iter": [5000]},
                                                       "optimizer_type": GridSearch,
                                                       "optimizer_params": {'cv': 10}
                                                       }

       In this case, the MLPR hyperparameters hidden_layer_sizes and alpha are to be
       optimized using a grid search. During training, six different combinations are tested: [64, 64,
       64] and 0.1; [64, 64, 64] and 0.3; [64, 64, 64] and 1.0; [128, 128, 128] and 0.1; [128, 128,
       128] and 0.3; and [128, 128, 128] and 1.0. For each combination, 10-fold cross-validation is
       used (cv of 10) and then the accuracies are compared, with the best selected.

       For a Bayesian search, you only need to specify the maximum and minimum values of the
       hyperparameters to search between. However, only scalar values can be optimized. For
       example:

          hyperparameters["Y0"] = {"model_type": MLPR,
                                                       "model_hyperparameters":
                                                       {"hidden_layer_sizes": [[64, 64, 64]],
                                                       "alpha": [0.1, 1.0],
                                                       "max_iter": [500, 5000]},
                                                       "optimizer_type": Bayesian,
                                                       "optimizer_params": {"cv": 5}}
                                                       }

       This optimizes the alpha and max_iter hyperparameters between the maximum and
       minimum values specified. A 5-fold cross-validation is used to assess which combination of
       hyperparameters is best.

       Each of the optimizer types has several parameters. For GridSearch, information about the
       parameters can be found at the scikit learn website [14]. For Bayesian, these can be found
       at [15].

      Encoding XY Profile Targets

       Curve data from different simulations might not share the same size of data points or the
       same range. To fit them with a single machine-learning training and prediction scheme,
       preprocessing is usually necessary. In addition to format considerations, preprocessing also
       contributes to the quality of a machine-learning model by extracting curve features or by
       reducing the signal bandwidth. The preprocessing of curve targets is performed by using
       encoders in the scwml module.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  61

W-2024.09
Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module
Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

       Currently, the only encoder that can be used for XY profile targets is the point sampling
       encoder. The point sampling encoder converts curve data into a certain number of data
       points by sampling at regular intervals. The encoder is set using the encoder_type model
       hyperparameter. A point sampling encoder that takes Y values at 50 evenly spaced X
       coordinates is set with:

          hyperparameters["XY_Profile"] = {...,
                                                                  "encoder_type": PointSamplingEncoder,
                                                                  "encoder_params": {"num_data_points": 50}

       The point sampling encoder has other parameters that can also be set:

       · num_data_points is the number of data points to sample from each curve.

       · use_floating_range specifies whether to clip off leading and trailing zeroes for the
            curves, potentially making the effective range of various curves different. By default,
            use_floating_range=False. If the X axis is not the exact same for all samples, then
            use_floating_range must be set to True.

       · sort_axis sorts data using a specified axis; (-1, 0, 1) is available. The default
            sort_axis=-1 means that data is not sorted.

       · remove_spikes removes singular point spikes such as [0, nonzero, 0]. By default,
            remove_spikes=False.

       Note: Only XY data that can be represented as a function y(x) can be encoded using the
            PointSamplingEncoder.

       Note: XY Profiles can only be used as targets in the Mx1 configuration.

      Saving and Loading Models

       While some of the tasks in mlcal automatically save and/or load Model objects, it is also
       possible to save or load models manually. A model can be saved using the save method:

          model_obj.save("model_filename.ml")

       A Model object can be loaded using the load method:

          model_obj.load("model_filename.ml")

       However, if model.obj.validate = True, then all the hyperparameters of the model to be
       loaded must match those of the current model. Otherwise, an error occurs. To avoid this, set
       model_obj.validate = False. The validate property can be set during model initialization
       as well.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  62

W-2024.09
Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module
Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

      Custom Models

       In addition to the models provided by the scwmal module, labeled by the keywords, you can
       pass a customized model as the main machine-learning model, provided that it has methods
       and interfaces conforming to the scikit-learn syntax:

       · A fit method takes two 2D NumPy arrays, representing the feature matrix and the output
            matrix, with a row (first dimension) for each sample/experiment, and a column (second
            dimension) for each input feature or output target value. The return value for the fit
            method is optional.

       · A predict method takes one 2D NumPy array, representing the feature matrix, with a row
            (first dimension) for each sample/experiment, and a column (second dimension) for each
            input feature. Its return value is a NumPy matrix containing the predicted target values,
            with a row (first dimension) for each sample.

       Therefore, in Python, a custom model class has a minimum set of methods of:

          import numpy as np
          from numpy.typing import NDArray

          class CustomModel:
                 def __init__(self, args):
                        #Initialize the model
                 def fit(self, X: NDArray, Y: NDArray) -> None | NDArray
                        #Train the model using feature inputs X and target values Y
                 def predict(self, X: NDArray) -> NDArray:
                        #Return predicted target values for X features

      General MLCal Task Use and Format

       Tasks in the mlcal application are all member functions of a specific instance of the mlcal
       object and are called by:

          mlcal_obj.task(arguments)

       Most of these tasks contain the following arguments:

       · models (list): Specifies a list of trained machine-learning Model objects

       · parameters (list): Specifies a list of machine-learning model Parameter objects

       · targets (list): Specifies a list of machine-learning model Target objects

       · parent (string): Specifies the parent project. If set to None, then option("parent") is
            used.

       · child_dir (string): Specifies the directory of the child projects. If set to None, then
            option("child_dir") is used.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  63

W-2024.09
Chapter 3: Sentaurus Calibration Workbench Machine-Learning Module
Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal

· save_report (bool): Specifies whether to save a report in the node report folder
    ({project_dir}/results/nodes/{node number}/n{node number}_report/)

For the sake of organization, it is typical to group all the models, parameters, and targets into
lists after they have been defined. These lists are then passed as arguments into the
individual tasks. For example:

 models = [ml_0, ml_1]
 parameters = [p_0, p_1, p_2]
 targets = [t_0, t_1]
 mlcal_obj.task(models=models, parameters=parameters,

                             targets=targets)

This is the format that is used throughout the rest of this chapter.

Handling Conditions

Parameter, Target, and Model each have arguments for defining conditions. For example,
Parameter with choice argument becomes a conditional (discrete) parameter, and Target
and Model have the conditions argument. The use cases vary depending on the
combination of these three elements, as shown in the two tables that follow. If there are split
conditions, it is recommended to use (Conditional parameters, Force pass, Target condition,
Model condition) = (T, T, Defined, Undefined) or (T, T, Defined, Defined) in general. It is also
recommended that the values of the three conditions should be same.

Table 7    Task dependent condition priority

Task name          Priority of conditions

create_ml          Model conditions, conditional parameters

generate_data_ml   Conditional parameters

predict_ml         Conditional parameters

reverse_search_ml  Target conditions, conditional parameters (If force_pass is True, only
                   the initial value is used. If force_pass is False, it become a discrete
                   parameter.)
