Cleaning Up Projects and Workflows

You can clean up projects and workflows.
To clean up a project or workflow:
1. Select a project or workflow in the Projects panel.
2. Choose File > Clean Up Project/Workflow.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                         145

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Setting Up a Calibration Flow

            Alternatively, right-click the selected project or workflow and choose Clean Up.
            The Cleanup dialog box opens.

3. Select the options as required:

Option              Description

SCW GUI Project     Remove all data that has been saved in the Sentaurus Calibration
                    Workbench project: the run state of the project, workflow and steps, and
                    the data obtained from Sentaurus Calibration Workbench (cells in pale
                    orange), and substeps.

SCW Project         Remove all Sentaurus Calibration Workbench project files. This is
                    equivalent to the Sentaurus Workbench cleanup of a Sentaurus
                    Calibration Workbench project.

ML Models           Remove machine-learning model files created by any machine-learning
                    function or module.

Database            Remove data saved by Sentaurus Calibration Workbench to be read by
                    the Sentaurus Calibration Workbench GUI, using a database
                    infrastructure.

SWB Child Projects  Remove all child projects created by Sentaurus Calibration Workbench.
                    These projects are located in the folder specified either by the option
                    child_dir (see Defining the Child Project Directory on page 34) or by
                    the task argument child_dir.

4. Click Clean Up.

Setting Up a Calibration Flow

       This section describes how to define a basic calibration flow using the Sentaurus Calibration
       Workbench GUI, following the setup explained in Chapter 2 on page 20.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                                  146

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Setting Up a Calibration Flow

       Setting up a calibration flow requires the following steps:
       1. Create a project and select the calibration module to use. See Creating a Project and

            Selecting the Calibration Module.
       2. Define the main calibration options on the Options subtab of the Task tab for the Init

            step. See Setting Up the Calibration Input.
       3. Add parameters, conditions, and targets to the Init step. See Setting Up the Calibration

            Input. In addition:
            a. Import parameters from Sentaurus Workbench projects as condition parameters. See

                Importing Data From Sentaurus Workbench Projects.
            b. Convert condition parameters to calibration parameters and vice versa. See

                Converting Condition Parameters to Calibration Parameters, and Calibration
                Parameters to Condition Parameters.
       4. Add new steps, assigning a task to each one. Set up the task arguments and options.
            See Setting Up the Calibration Input.
       5. Execute the calibration flow. See Executing the Calibration Flow.

      Creating a Project and Selecting the Calibration Module

       You can select a calibration module when you create a Sentaurus Calibration Workbench
       GUI project and when additional workflows are added to an existing project.
       To create a project:
       1. In the Projects panel, click the + button.

            The Add New Project dialog box opens.

2. Enter the name of the project.
3. Select a calibration module for the project to use.
4. Click Confirm.
The new project contains one workflow with one step, the Init step (see Figure 8).

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                        147

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Setting Up a Calibration Flow

Setting Up the Calibration Input

Calibration input includes parameters, targets, and the simulation flow (calibration steps)
and its options.

Note:
    In the Sentaurus Calibration Workbench GUI, parameters (calibration
    parameters), conditions (condition parameters), targets (extracted variable
    targets), and function targets are defined only in the Init step.

Options

You must define the parent and tools task options in the Init step. You also can define
several optional calibration controls. All of these options are located on the Options subtab
of the Task tab.

Some controls such as parent, child_dir, and tools are defined in Setting Options for
Calibrations on page 33.

Parameters

Each parameter (calibration parameter) is a row in the table. To add parameters on the
Parameters tab, click Add. After a new row is added, you must define a name for the
parameter and assign an initial value, which is mandatory. See Parameters Tab on
page 135.

The name of the parameter must correspond to a parameter name as defined in the
Sentaurus Workbench project (case sensitive) and the parameter in the simulator command
file or parameter file. You must use the Sentaurus Workbench parameter syntax (@name@) for
parameters in parameterized files.

You can include or exclude parameters from a calibration task (step) by selecting or clearing
the check box next to the parameter name.

Conditions

Each condition (condition parameter) is a row in the table. To add conditions on the
Conditions tab, click Add. After a new row is added, you must define a name for the
condition and assign a value. See Conditions Tab on page 136.

The conditions are used in targets as split conditions.

Targets

Each target is a row in the table. To add targets on the Targets tab, click Add. After a new
row is added, you must define a name for the target and assign a target value, which can be

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  148

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Setting Up a Calibration Flow

       a scalar for a scalar target or a reference target xy profile (one of them must be defined). See
       Targets Tab on page 137.

       The name of the target must correspond to the name of a Sentaurus Workbench extracted
       variable (case sensitive).

       You can include or exclude targets from a calibration task (step) by selecting or clearing the
       check box next to the target name.

       If split conditions (condition parameters that can have different values) are defined, then you
       can use them by setting them in the Conditions column.

      Function Targets

       Each function target is a row in the table. To add targets on the Function Targets tab, click
       Add. After a new row is added, you must define a name for the function target and assign a
       target scalar value, as well as the extracted variable targets and the function to be used
       (extracted variable targets must be defined before function targets). See Function Targets
       Tab on page 139.

       You can include or exclude function targets from a calibration task (step) by selecting or
       clearing the check box next to the name of the function target.

      Tasks

       Setting up tasks (steps) defines the calibration flow.

       To add a task:

       1. In the Projects panel, right-click the corresponding workflow and choose Add New Step
            to Workflow.

            The Add New Step dialog box opens.

2. Enter the name of the step.
    If the Step Name is not defined, then it takes the same name as the task.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                   149

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Setting Up a Calibration Flow

       3. Select a task.

            Each module has a list of valid tasks that can be added as calibration steps.

       4. Click Confirm.

       The step is added to the end of the workflow.

       After a task is added, you can define its options as follows:

       · If the task is custom, then you can define a Python script to be executed in that task.

       · For any other task type, you can define the arguments of that task, as well as the
            prologue and epilogue commands (of that task) together with their arguments.

      Importing Data From Sentaurus Workbench Projects

       You can import calibration parameters, condition parameters and targets from the data
       defined in Sentaurus Workbench projects. It is expected that you import this data from the
       parent project used.

       The Sentaurus Workbench parameters can be imported as calibration parameters
       (Parameters tab) or condition parameters (Conditions tab), with all the values used in the
       given project. Also, it is possible to use the extracted variables from the Sentaurus
       Workbench project to create targets (Targets tab) automatically, with the selected condition
       parameters.

       You can import Sentaurus Workbench parameters in one of the following ways.

       To import Sentaurus Workbench parameters as condition parameters:

       1. In the Sentaurus Calibration Workbench GUI, choose File > Import SWB Data.

            The Find SWB Project dialog box opens.

            Select the Sentaurus Workbench project to be used and click Choose.

            Note:
                If the parent project is defined, then the dialog box has that directory selected.
                If the parent project is not defined, then the dialog box has the current project
                directory selected.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  150

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Setting Up a Calibration Flow

            The Import SWB Data dialog box opens.

2. Highlight the required Sentaurus Workbench parameters in the left pane and move them
    to the Imported Parameters or Imported Conditions pane.

3. If you want to import Targets, enable the Targets section at the right side of the dialog
    box. If not, skip to Step 7.

4. Set Extracted variable to be used for the Target.

5. Set the Target value, if there is one in the parent project.

6. Optionally, you can define a target prefix for the given set of targets.

7. Click Import.

    A dialog box opens, displaying the summary of the operation.

Alternatively, to import all the parameters available in the given Sentaurus Workbench
project as condition parameters:

1. In the Projects panel, right-click the selected project and choose Import All SWB Data.

    The Find SWB Project dialog box opens.

2. Select the Sentaurus Workbench project to be used.

    Note:
        If the parent project is defined, then the dialog box has that directory selected.
        If the parent project is not defined, then the dialog box has the current project
        directory selected.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide       151

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Setting Up a Calibration Flow

      Converting Condition Parameters to Calibration Parameters, and
      Calibration Parameters to Condition Parameters

       You can convert condition parameters (Conditions tab) to calibration parameters
       (Parameters tab) and vice versa.

       To do so, right-click any column of a selected set of condition parameters (Conditions tab)
       and choose Convert Condition Into Parameter.

       Note:
            Only numeric condition parameter values can be converted to calibration
            parameters (Parameters tab).

       The new calibration parameters do not have their lower and upper bounds set, so you must
       set them manually.

       On the other hand, to convert a calibration parameter (Parameters tab) to a condition
       parameter (Conditions tab), you must select the required calibration parameters and then
       right-click these parameters and choose Convert Parameter Into Condition.

       The new condition parameters have only one value assigned in the Choices column, with
       the same value as the initial value of the calibration parameter.

      Executing the Calibration Flow

       After you have defined the calibration flow, you execute the corresponding calibration by
       clicking the Play button in the Projects panel.

       This action launches the Sentaurus Calibration Workbench batch tool (binary scw) as a job
       in the queue defined on the command line (default: local:default). The status of the
       Sentaurus Calibration Workbench GUI is updated at each step, including the last calculated
       values of parameters and targets.

       You can also run jobs in a different queue by right-clicking the project or workflow and
       choosing Run (Project or Workflow) Calibration, and then selecting the required queue.

       You can also change the queue in the GUI preferences (see Customizing GUI Preferences
       on page 145).

       To check the status of steps, refer the box next to the step name to the legend in the Projects
       panel.

       On the Parameters, Targets, and Function Targets tabs, the last values calculated are
       shown in cells with an orange background (see Figure 22).

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  152

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Plots

Figure 22 Projects panel and Tables panel showing a running project

Plots

       This section describes different types of plot and how to create them in the Sentaurus
       Calibration Workbench GUI.
       You also can create plots directly from the tables (quick plots).

     Creating Curve Plots From Scalar Data

       You can create curve plots from data represented as a single scalar value per step, which
       means that the bottom (x) axis represents the step or sub-step number, and the y (left) axis
       represents the scalar data in a single cell from one table.
       An example of this type of data is the RMS error of the target obtained as the result of the
       execution of the step (calibration task). You also can create curve plots from the total RMS
       error (all targets).
       To create a curve plot from scalar data:
       1. Choose Plots > New Plots.

           The Plot Creation Wizard opens.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                         153

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Plots

       2. On the Plot Type Selection page, select Curve Plot.

3. Click Next.

4. On the Input Data Selection page, select the data source from which to create the curve
    plot.

    Select a Step or Sub-Step (if required) or both.

    From the Step list, select one of the following:

     If you select a specific step, then the plot will display the same data, independent of
        the step selected in the Projects panel.

     If you select SELECTED (for curve data), then the plot will show the step data
        corresponding to the step selected in the Projects panel.

     If you select ALL (only for scalar data), then the plot will use all the steps to create the
        plot, using a single value per step.

        Scalar data curve plots are not dependent of the step selection in the Projects panel.

    In this case, select ALL to create a curve plot from scalar data.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                   154

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Plots

            From the Source list, select data source: Parameters or Targets.

5. Click Next.

6. On the Elements Selection page, select the required elements to plot.

    The Targets list always shows the total RMS error and all the available targets. The
    Columns list shows only the columns or attributes that can be scalar.

    From the Targets list, select all the targets. From the Columns list, select RMS Error.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                  155

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Plots

            This selection will plot the RMS error of all targets together with the total one in each
            step.

7. Click Next.

8. On the Curves Setup page, set up all the curves to be created.

    Usually, the number of curves is the number of combinations of the selections made on
    the Elements Selection page between the Targets and Columns lists.

    Set the name of the curves, the target plot, the color, the line style and width, and the
    marker style and size.

    You can create multiple curves with different curves by changing the name of the plot in
    the Plot column.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  156

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Plots

9. Click Next.

10.On the Plots Setup page, set the visibility of the curve legend and the axes properties of
    the plots to be created.

    Axes properties are the title, the visibility, the scaling (log or linear), the units, the grid
    visibility, and the ranges (minimum and maximum) to be used.

    Note:
        You cannot change the plot name because it is used as a reference for the
        target plots of the curves to be created from the Curves Setup page.

Creating Quick Plots

To create a quick plot:

1. In a table, select the cells with the required information to generate a plot.

2. Right-click the selected cells and choose New Quick Plot.

    If plots have already been generated, then there is an option to add the requested curves
    into any of those plots.

You can also right-click in the Total RMS Error field, on the Targets or Function Targets
tab, and choose Quick Plot to create a plot from this data.

Using this feature, curves are formatted automatically according to the following rules:

· Each color is assigned, in order of curve creation, to the specific element of the tables
    (row), so every curve created from the same element, but different column, has the same
    color in a plot.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  173

W-2024.09
Chapter 8: Graphical User Interface
Plots

       · On the Parameters tab, calibration parameter elements have the following format:
             The value has a solid line style, with a width of 2.
             The initial value has a dash line style, with a width of 1.
             The range (Min and Max columns) has a dot line style, with a width of 2.

       · On the Targets tab, target elements have the following format:
             The scalar or simulated profile value has a solid line style, with a width of 2.
             The source profile has circles as markers of size 7, without any line.
             The target scalar value has a dot line style, with a width of 1, and circles as markers
                of size 5.
             The RMS error value has a dash line style, with a width of 2.
             The total RMS error has a solid line style, with a width of 2, and is dark gray.

       · On the Function Targets tab, function target elements have the following format:
             The scalar value has a solid line style, with a width of 2.
             The target scalar value has a dot line style, with a width of 1, and circles as markers
                of size 5.
             The RMS error value has a dash line style, with a width of 2.
             The total RMS error has a solid line style, with a width of 2, and is dark gray.

9

9Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology
(AImage)

       This chapter describes how to use the Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology tool to
       analyze images with computer vision and machine learning algorithms.

Introduction to AImage

     Overview of Functionality

       Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage) is a software tool integrated with
       Sentaurus Calibration Workbench that extracts information from images. Information
       includes measurements, features, segments, profiles. AImage supports advanced computer
       vision and machine learning algorithms for pattern recognition. AImage is primarily used in
       TCAD to analyze TEM/SEM images, but it also works with general purpose images
       (grayscale or color RGB).

     Concepts

       · Computer vision: The field that focuses in acquiring, processing, analyzing, and
           understanding digital images. The goal is to extract explainable data from the real world.
           The term computer vision is typically interchangeable with Image Analysis or Image
           Processing.

       · Image: A visual representation. A digital image is composed of picture elements, also
           known as pixels, each with finite, discrete quantities of numeric representation for its
           intensity.

       · Transmission Electron Microscopy (TEM): A microscopy technique in which a beam
           of electrons is transmitted through a specimen to form an image. The specimen is most
           often an ultrathin section with less than 100-nm thickness or a suspension on a grid.

       · Scanning Electron Microscopy (SEM): A type of electron microscope that produces
           images of a sample by scanning the surface with a focused beam of electrons. The

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                                175

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Introduction to AImage

    electrons interact with atoms in the sample, producing various signals that contain
    information about the surface topography and composition of the sample.

· TEM Versus SEM: The main difference between SEM and TEM is that SEM creates an
    image by detecting reflected or knocked-off electrons, while TEM uses transmitted
    electrons (electrons that are passing through the sample) to create an image. As a
    result, TEM offers valuable information on the inner structure of the sample, such as
    crystal structure, morphology, and stress state information, while SEM provides
    information on the sample's surface and its composition.

· Segmentation: The representation of an image into something that is more meaningful
    and easier to analyze. The representation tries to classify the pixels in regions that
    belong to a class.

· Measurement: The quantification of attributes of an object or event. In computer vision,
    measurements quantify the regions or objects inside an image. The measurements are
    expressed as distances, areas, angles, or polygons.

· Contour Extraction: The process that obtains the boundary of a region or object in the
    image. The boundary is represented as a curve or polygon joining all the continuous
    points along the boundary.

     Features

       AImage compress a set of features to solve image analysis based on measurements and
       segmentations. See Table 16 for a detailed list of features.

Group      Description

           GUI (objects tree, viewer, algorithms, console, log)
           Open/Save project files
           Multiple application windows

              Image supports calibration: scaling, origin, and orientation

              Batch processing of multiple images

              Segmentations (masks with transparencies)

              Processing algorithms:

              · Resample: crop, padding, resize, translate, rotate
              · Intensity: cast, rescale, RGB to grayscale
              · Contour extraction: image or segmentation based
              · Filtering: Gaussian, mean, median, gradient
              · Arithmetic: add, subtract, multiply, divide

Introduction to Image Analysis

       Image analysis is the process of extracting information from images. This section elaborates
       on some concepts to better understand the underlying computations and algorithms in
       image processing.

Image Intensity

An image is a visual representation of something. A digital image is represented as an
n-dimensional array of pixel values. In 2D is a functional representation of intensity values.
The functional description of a grayscale image is f: [x,y]  [0, 255], with 0 being black and

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                           178

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Introduction to Image Analysis

255 being white (see Figure 26). A color image (RGB) has three channels. Therefore, in a
color image f(x,y) is a vector of three values instead of one. See Figure 27

Figure 26  Gray scale image. The corresponding intensity values are shown in a 2D array.
           Source: https://ai.stanford.edu/~syyeung/cvweb/tutorial1.html.

Figure 27  RGB or color image. (Upper) An image has three components for red, green, and
           blue colors. (Lower) The corresponding intensity values are shown in a 2D array
           with a vector representing a single intensity value. Source: https://ai.stanford.edu/
           ~syyeung/cvweb/tutorial1.html.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide               179

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Introduction to Image Analysis

      Image Calibration

       Image calibration is the process of matching dimensions and coordinates from the image
       space to some specific units (usually world coordinates with metric units). For 2D calibration,
       the image is transformed from pixel coordinates to world coordinates with the z coordinate
       assumed to be 0 (see Figure 28). The transformation requires a scaling 2d-vector, an origin
       2d-vector, and an orientation 2x2 matrix. The scaling defines the correspondence pixel per
       length (meter). The origin represents the zero coordinates for the image. The orientation
       determines the image rotation with respect to the origin. For simplicity, the calibration
       process often ignores the orientation.

Figure 28 Correspondence between image coordinate system and world coordinates

The conversion from pixel coordinates (u,v) to world coordinates (x,y) is defined as shown
in Equation 1.

           Transforming image pixel coordinates to world coordinates  (1)

            x  sx 0  Cos -Sin  u tx
           y =  0  sy  Sin                    Cos    v   +  ty
                                                            
           

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                          180

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Introduction to Image Analysis

       Where the scaling factors are expressed as (sx, sy). For digital images, the scaling on each
       axis is usually the same, that is, a squared area or squared field of view. The orientation
       matrix depends on the angle  in 2D and usually takes the value 0. The origin is expressed
       as (tx, ty).

Image Segmentation

Image segmentation is the process of partitioning a digital image into multiple image
segments, also known as image regions or image objects (sets of pixels). The segmentation
is also called a mask image. A segmentation is represented as f: [x,y]  [0, 1], with pixel
values 1 where the object of interest is and 0 everywhere else. There are multiple terms and
approaches shared between object detection and segmentation tasks. Figure 29
compresses and explains the differences between the terminology.

Figure 29  Image segmentation. Source: http://cs231n.stanford.edu/slides/2022/
           lecture_9_jiajun.pdf

· Classification is the process of labeling the object inside an image.

· Localization refers to the process of defining a bounding box where the classified object
    is.

· Object detection implies classification and localization of objects inside an image.

· Semantic segmentation is an approach detecting regions, for every pixel, the belonging
    class.

· Instance segmentation is an approach that identifies, for every pixel, the specific
    belonging instance of the object and can be described as semantic segmentation
    combined with classification.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                    181

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Features of the User Interface

      Contour Extraction

       Contours can be explained simply as a curve joining all the continuous points (along the
       boundary), having the same color or intensity. The contours are a useful tool for shape
       analysis and object detection and recognition. In computer vision, contour extraction
       requires to be automated and mathematically well defined. The automation is achieved by
       first detecting the regions and then extracting the edges, boundaries, or contours. Image
       segmentation algorithms are a suitable solution for region detection. The contours are better
       represented by a polygon with a set of points P: [x1, x2, ... xf].

Features of the User Interface

       The graphical user interface (GUI) of AImage offers a main window as shown in Figure 30.
       The GUI has a menu, toolbars, panels, and a viewer area. The panels' location can be
       moved around the GUI according to your preferences.

       · Menu: The menu offers all the functionalities of AImage. The menu is available at the top
            of the window.

       · Toolbars: The toolbars contain quick access buttons to create objects and apply
            algorithms in AImage. The toolbars are in the upper part of the window and below the
            menu.

       · Image Processing Panel: This panel allows you to switch image processing algorithms
            and apply them to image objects. The panel is located on the right side of the window.

       · Object Panel: It shows the working data objects in AImage. The panel is located to the
            upper-right side of the window.

       · Properties Panel: This panel shows the properties of the current object selected in the
            object panel. The panel is located on the lower-right of the window.

       · Developer Panel: This panel contains the development tools including a Python console
            with log and script records. The panel is in the bottom part of the window.

       · Viewer Area: This is the display area for images and measurements. The panel is in the
            central area of the window.

     Manual: Edit segmentation manually.

        segmentation.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  184

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Features of the User Interface

             Threshold: Algorithm for setting a threshold value to segment an image.
             Segment Anything: Apply the Segment Anything algorithm to an image.
             Train Model: Method to train a segmentation model using a data set of images and

                segmentations.
             Predict Model: Use trained model to segment an image.
       · Filter: Common filters for image processing.
             Gaussian: Apply a Gaussian filter to an image.
             Mean: Apply a mean filter to an image.
             Median: Apply a median filter to an image.
             Gradient: Compute the gradient of an image.
       · Intensity: Image intensity algorithms.
             Cast: Convert the pixel type for numerical precision.
             Rescale: Rescale image intensity values.
             RGB to Gray: Convert a RGB or RGBA image to gray scale image.
             Gray to 3 Channels: Convert a gray scale image to 3 channels. It replicates the

                intensity values in the other channels.
       · Arithmetic: Apply an arithmetic operation between image - constant or between two

            images.
             Addition: Pixelwise addition of an image with another image or a constant.
             Subtract: Pixelwise subtraction of an image with another image or a constant.
             Multiply: Pixelwise multiplication of an image with another image or a constant.
             Divide: Pixelwise division of an image with another image or a constant.
       · Resample: Transform images and resample.
             Padding: Pad an image with additional pixels.
             Crop: Crop image size.
             Resize: Change the pixel size of an image.
             Translate: Translate an image defined by a point location.
             Rotate: Image rotation defined by an angle.
             Rotate about a point: Define a central point and an angle of rotation for an image.

Objects Panel

The Objects panel holds an object tree. The tree contains the working and existing objects
in AImage. AImage defines a hierarchy where image objects are at the top level and all the
measurements (distance, angle, polygon, bounding box) and segmentation objects are

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  188

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Features of the User Interface

under the image at the lower level. That means that images are the parent object and
measurement are child objects.

The object tree has for column is described as:

· Name: Object name. Can be changed using the context menu (Right-click > Rename) or
    by double-clicking the name and then editing the text.

· Type: Object type. Fixed parameters.

· Visible: Check box to hide or show the object in the viewer.

· Color: Object color. Can be changed using left click on the color area. It shows a new
    window form to select the desired color.

The object tree allows drag and drop.

Figure 40  Objects panel. The listed objects are images, segmentations, distances, angles,

The viewer allows you to interact with images and measurement objects. When you click an
object in the viewer, the object is highlighted in the object panel (tree). The same behavior
occurs if an object is selected in the object panel (tree): The object is highlighted in the
viewer area. The viewer interaction changes depending on the selected object.

Interaction With Images and Segmentations

The images are zoomed in/out with the mouse wheel or by right-clicking along with up and
down movements. The image center can be displaced by holding the middle mouse button
and then moving in the desired direction.

Interaction With Measurements

There are two interaction modes for measurement objects. The first mode is placement, and
the second mode is editing. The placement is when a new measurement is created using
the measurement toolbar. The editing is related to changes of existing objects.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide               191

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Features of the User Interface

      Interaction With Distance and Angle Objects

       The distance objects are placed using the Distance Measurement button in the
       measurement toolbar. The distance is created with one left click. Afterward, the created
       object is displayed in the object panel. Left-clicking again inside the image locates the first
       point of the distance object. The third left-click finishes the distance placement. During
       placement, it is possible to perform all the interactions described in Interaction With Images
       and Segmentations on page 191.

       Distance objects allow locking movement in the horizontal direction or in the vertical
       direction during placement and editing. To lock in the horizontal direction, use the H key. For
       vertical lock, use the V key. To reset the locked movement, use the R key or the same key
       that locked the movement.

       The editing for distance objects start when the object is selected or highlighted from the
       Viewer or the Objects panel. Holding the mouse left-click over the end points (displayed as
       a cross) allows movement editing over the distance object. After releasing the mouse
       button, the object updates the coordinates values.

       You can create angle objects using the Angle Measurement button in a similar way to
       distance objects. In this case, the number of left clicks is four, because the angle objects
       have three coordinates. The interaction for placement and editing work as with the distance
       objects.

      Interaction With Polygons

       The polygons are placed using the Polygon Measurement button in the measurement
       toolbar. The polygon is created with one left click over the viewer area. Then, each point
       coordinate of the polygon is created with one left click over the desired location. To finish the
       placement, right-click over the final point coordinate.

       You can start editing a polygon by highlighting the object from the Viewer or the Objects
       panel. The polygon's points can be moved individually by placing the mouse near a point
       and holding the left mouse button and dragging over to the new desired location. The entire
       polygon can shift his location by approaching to one of the polygon's points and holding the
       middle mouse button while dragging and dropping to the new polygon location. You can
       delete a point of the polygon by selecting the desired point in the viewer and then pressing
       the Delete key.

      Interaction With Bounding Boxes

       You can generate bounding box objects using the Bounding Box Measurement button in
       the Measurement toolbar. The bounding box placement works as drawing a rectangle.
       Compared to the previously described objects, there is no need of an initial left-click to
       create the object. The drawing starts left-clicking over the upper-left corner in the image,

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  192

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Features of the User Interface

       holding the mouse button, then dragging to the lower-right corner and dropping in the
       desired location.

       You can edit the bounding box by moving the mouse near the boundaries of the box. The
       mouse cursor changes its shape, and you can then hold the left mouse button and move to
       the new desired location. Placing the mouse over the center of the box allows you to shift
       the box location by dragging and dropping with the mouse middle button.

Working With Segmentations

Segmentations are images that contain pixel values of 1 where the object of interest is
located and 0 everywhere else (semantic segmentation). A segmentation always has an
image as a parent object. The segmentation has the same pixel size and coordinate system
as the parent image.

To create a segmentation using algorithms or manual editing, see Image Segmentation on
page 181.

Image Metrology

Image metrology is the process of measuring length (in metric units) of the existing shapes,
regions, or objects in an image. Image calibration changes the image coordinates to world
coordinates in metric units using Equation 1.

In AImage, the image calibration is the recommended first step before making
measurements. However, AImage allows you to perform calibration at any point during
processing an image while keeping consistency in the measurement's locations. This
means that the measurement object such as distances or polygons can be placed first and
then the image can be calibrated.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                                199

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Image Processing With AImage

To manually calibrate an image:

Method          Procedure

Python console  # Having an image object called Image1
                from aimage.core import Image
                Image1 = Image.from_file("<file_path>")
                Image1.calibrate(unit = "nm", spacing = (2.0, 1.0), origin =
                (100.0, 50.0))

GUI             Select the image object to calibrate in the Objects panel (tree), click the

                Image menu, select Calibration, click Manual, set the parameters: units,

                width, height, origin x, and origin y, and click Apply.

Microscopic images (TEM/SEM) typically contain a ruler that specify the correspondence
between pixel and length. AImage allows the user interactive calibration to place a distance
object to calculate the proper calibration values. The procedure is shown in Figure 48 and
involves the following:

1. Select the image object to calibrate in the Objects panel (tree).

2. Click the Image menu.

3. Click Interactive.

4. Select Calibration.

5. Set the parameters: units, measure length, length [metric], measure origin.

6. Click Apply.

The Measure length button changes the viewer interaction similar to when you place a
distance object. Click the image to set the first point (usually the left side of the ruler) and the
click again to set the final point of the measurement. Edit to fine-tune the measurement
points. After the ruler is placed, click the Measure length button again to finish the
procedure. This updates the Length [pixels] according to the measure. See Figure 49.

The origin can be measured interactively as the length. Use the Measure origin button to
start the interaction. Left-click on the desired location for the image center. The interaction in
the viewer creates a green cross object in the image having its center at the desired location.
The center can be moved by dragging and dropping the horizontal or the vertical line of the
cross. To finish, click the Measure origin button again and this updates the parameters
"Origin x" and "Origin y".

After setting the length and the origin (or simply one of them), use the Apply button to finish
the interactive calibration. Figure 50 shows the measurement objects created after image
calibration. These objects reflect the correct measurements in metric units according to the
ruler.

Applying Image Algorithms

Most of the image algorithms available in AImage work in a similar way. The procedure
involves the selection of the algorithm in the Image menu, which displays an algorithm form
in the Image Processing Panel. After setting the parameter value, you run the algorithm
using the Apply button. As an example, the following description summarize how to use a
Gaussian filter with an image:

Method          Procedure

Python console  # Having an image object called Image1
                from aimage.vision import filters
                Image2 = filters.gaussian(image = Image1, variance = 0,
                radius = 3)

GUI             Having images in the Objects panel (tree), click the Image menu, select

                Filter, click Gaussian, set the parameters: input image, variance, radius,

                output image, and click Apply.

The input image is a combo box listing each of the images in the Objects panel (tree) and
has the option to select as input "All" the images. The option "All" means that if the algorithm
is applied, it performs the same operation on every image in the Objects panel. After
selecting the option "All", click Apply to effectively run the algorithm.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                             202

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Image Processing With AImage

       The output image parameter is a combo box with two options: "New", "Inplace". The option
       "New" means that the algorithm generates a new image after being applied while the option
       "Inplace" means that the input image is going to be replaced after the algorithm runs.

       After applying any image algorithm in the GUI, the script record shows the corresponding
       Python code to perform the same operation. Therefore, reproducing image algorithm
       workflows is straightforward by saving the script records.

Image Segmentation

Image segmentation generates a mask image. In AImage, the segmentations are child
objects to images. There are multiple methods in AImage to achieve manual or automatic
image segmentation.

Manual Segmentation

You can perform manual segmentation in AImage using the Manual Segmentation button
in the Segmentation toolbar. This action opens the Manual Segmentation form in the Image
Processing Panel as shown in Figure 51. The manual segmentation operates on the
currently selected image in the Objects panel (tree). The parameter options for the manual
segmentation are:

· Select (segmentation): Combo box to select the segmentation to edit. It has the option
    "New segmentation" or the names of existing segmentations in the working image
    (Image1 in Figure 51).

· Pen: Tool to add pixels to the segmentation.

· Erase: Tool to remove pixels to the segmentation.

· Shape (Square or Circle): Select the shape of the editing interaction. This changes the
    mouse drawing shape during the interaction in the viewer.

· Area [%]: Select the area proportional to the viewer field of view that covers the pen or
    erase tool.

· Editing (display only): Show the current segmentation under editing after pressing the
    edit button.

· Edit: Start editing the segmentation.

The manual segmentation process starts after pressing the Edit button. The interaction
works like other pixel painting software. If the pen tool is activated, the left mouse button
adds pixels of the segmentation inside the selected area. If the erase tool is activated, the
left mouse button removes pixels of the segmentation inside the selected area. Zooming
and the image interactions are enabled during editing (see Interaction With Images and
Segmentations on page 191).

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  203

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Image Processing With AImage

Figure 51 Manual Segmentation parameters form in Image Processing Panel

Thresholding Segmentation

Thresholding segmentation uses the image histogram (profile of intensity values) to
separate the background from the foreground. The threshold value is the intensity point that
splits the image regions and the resulting segment. The algorithm has other parameters
related to inside and outside values. The inside value defines the value that the
segmentation takes below the threshold and the outside value the above the threshold.
AImage also supports an automatic thresholding algorithm called Otsu.

To apply thresholding segmentation:

Method          Procedure

Python console  # Having an image object called Image1
                from aimage.vision import threshold
                Segmentation1 = threshold.binary(image = Image1,
                threshold_value = 128,
                inside_value = 0, outside_value = 1)

GUI             Select the image object to calibrate in the Objects panel (tree), click the

                Threshold Segmentation button in the Segmentation toolbar, set the

                parameters: inside value, outside value, threshold value, and click Apply.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                             204

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Image Processing With AImage

Segment Anything

The Segment Anything Model (SAM) is a segmentation model released by Facebook that is
publicly available [1]. SAM is a promptable segmentation system with zero-shot
generalization to unfamiliar objects and images, without the need for additional training [2].
SAM refers to the neural network model and the corresponding dataset (SA-1B) of 1B
masks and 11M images. SAM performs Instance Segmentation (Bounding box,
Classification, Semantic Segmentation). AImage implements SAM with a single button. The
input is an image, and the outputs are the semantic segmentations.

To obtain segmentations with SAM:

Method          Procedure
Python console
                # Having an image object called Image1
GUI             from aimage.vision import segment
                list_segmentations = segment.anything(image = Image1)

                Select the image object to calibrate in the Objectx panel (tree), click the
                Segment Anything button in the Segmentation toolbar. The algorithm
                starts running and produces the segmentations.

Figure 52 shows the result of applying the segment anything algorithm to the same image
shown in Figure 8. The image is a Gate All Around (GAA) transistor. The algorithm create
automatically segments for all the regions in the image.

Figure 52  Segment Anything algorithm applied to a GAA image. Source: https://
           mma.prnewswire.com/media/1504371/Row_of_2_nm_nanosheet_devices.jpg

Sometimes, the resulting segments created by segment anything are not representative of
the user needs. For this case, you can obtain the desired segmentation by combining or

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                    205

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Image Processing With AImage

       removing segments using binary operations (see Binary Toolbar on page 188) or manually
       editing segmentation.

      Training Segmentation Models

       AImage supports training machine learning models for image segmentation. The process
       requires a training dataset that needs to be created by the user. The objects panel in
       Figure 53 shows the dataset structure: each image in the data set must have at least one
       segmentation with the same name. This segmentation is interpreted as the ground truth
       data for training.

       To train a segmentation model, select the Segmentation option in the Image menu, and then
       select Train Model. The Image Processing Panel opens a form called Train Segmentation
       Model (see Figure 53). The parameters for training a segmentation model are:

       · Dataset folder: Folder to save the input dataset (images and segmentations).

       · Training folder: Folder to place the training metadata.

       · Network model: Select the network model UNET, VNET, or UNET-TR.

       · Image size: Image patch size used for training.

       · Epochs: Number of iterations during the optimization.

       · Learning rate: Parameter value to be adjusted during training to achieve convergence.

       · Batch size: Set the batch size during training.

       · Validation split: Sets the proportion of images used for validation. Value from 0 to 1
            (equivalent to 0% or 100%).

       · Overwrite model: Select to overwrite the output model file if the selected training folder
            already has a model.

       · Augmentations: Enable augmentations during training. This randomly translates and
            rotates the training images during every epoch.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  206

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Image Processing With AImage

Figure 53 Train segmentation models with AImage

The button Apply button starts the training process and two pop-up windows are displayed.
The first window shows the optimization curves and the second window shows the
prediction images during validation. The input parameters should be tuned to achieve
convergence and a good model. The recommended minimum number of images for training
a segmentation model is 10.

When the training process finishes, the log prints the output model file path. This model file
can be used to predict segmentations for new images. Select the Segmentation option in the
Image menu and then select the Predict Model option. This opens a new form in the
processing panel. The form only has one parameter "Model file" where the model path is
then introduced. The Apply button generates a segmentation for the image selected in the
object panel (tree).

You can create the training dataset by using a combination of manual segmentation,
thresholding, or segment anything. In addition, new images can also be incorporated and
use the trained model to generate an initial estimation of their ground truth segmentation.
This methodology is represented in Figure 54. The approach speeds up and reduces the
burden of the training data collection.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide               207

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Image Processing With AImage

Figure 54 Proposed ML pipeline to train segmentation models with AImage

     Contour Extraction

       AImage handles contour extraction as a two-step process. First, a semantic segmentation is
       obtained from an image and then the contour extraction algorithm generates a polygon
       measurement (see Figure 55). Using the segmentation as the primary source of the contour
       simplifies the computation due to the inherent binary representation of segmentation
       images. Furthermore, this procedure means that the most difficult part of the contour
       extraction process is to achieve a good semantic segmentation of the object.

Figure 55 How to apply contour extraction in AImage

To extract contours from a segmentation object:

Method          Procedure

Python console  # Having a segmentation object called Segmentation1
                from aimage.vision import contours
                list_polygons = contours.from_segmentation(Segmentation1)

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                             208

Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Image Processing With AImage

Method     Procedure
GUI
           Having an image and its segmentation in the Objects panel (tree), click
           the Image menu, select Contour, click Extraction from Segmentation,
           select the input segmentation, and click Apply.

Interaction With Sentaurus Calibration Workbench

There are two methods to achieve interaction between AImage and Sentaurus Calibration
Workbench. The straightforward way is to share objects between AImage and Sentaurus
Calibration Workbench using files. Contours extracted from AImage can be exported as plx
files and then imported as targets in Sentaurus Calibration Workbench.

The other alternative is direct sharing and works only when AImage is opened from
Sentaurus Calibration Workbench GUI. For an existing measurement in AImage, the context
menu (right-click the object) in the Objects panel (tree) has the "Link to SCW variable". The
submenu displays the existing target variables names in Sentaurus Calibration Workbench
and select one. This variable linking enables that the AImage measurement shares the
coordinate data with Sentaurus Calibration Workbench.

Scripting

You can run python scripts with AImage using the option -s (--script). The algorithm
runs first and then the GUI opens. To finish the script after running, use the --offscreen
option to avoid opening the GUI. The python scripts mainly import aimage components, but
it is possible to use any Python library supported in the TCAD Sentaurus tool.

Scripting Example

A simple script to read an image is shown in the following script. AImage can run the script
from the command line:

 $ aimage -s read_image.py

Script 1: read_image.py script. Reads an image and then applies image calibration.

 from aimage.core import Image

 Image1 = Image.from_file("/user/name/images/sample.jpg")
                  # import image from file

 Image1.calibrate(unit = "nm", spacing = (0.7, 0.7), origin = (210, 122))
                  # calibrate image

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide               209

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
Image Processing With AImage

Script With Arguments

AImage supports having additional inputs through the command line for automation or batch
processing. This means that the input files are not hardcoded in the script. For this, the
option "script-args" is used after the script file name. The input parameters are added to
the script code using the argparse library. See the example to achieve contour extraction in
the script that follows.

AImage can run the script from the command line:

 $ aimage -s contour_extraction.py script-args -i <input_image> -o
                       <output_file_prefix>

Script 2: Script contour_extraction.py. Reads an input image as command line argument,
then extracts contours as polygons and saves them as text files.

 #!/usr/bin/env python
 from argparse import ArgumentParser

 from aimage.core import Image, Polygon
 from aimage.vision import intensity
 from aimage.vision import threshold
 from aimage.vision import contours

def parse_arguments():
       """Parse input arguments"""
       parser = ArgumentParser()
       parser.add_argument(
              "-i", "--input", type=str, required=True,
              help="Input image file"
       )

     parser.add_argument(
              "-o", "--output", type=str, default="polygon",
              help="Output file prefix"

     )
     args = parser.parse_args()
     return args

def main():
       args = parse_arguments()

       img1 = Image.from_file(args.input) # create image from file.
       img2 = intensity.rgb2gray(img1)

                    # if image is rgb convert to grayscale
       img3 = threshold.otsu(img2) # otsu segmentation algorithm
       plgs = contours.from_segmentation(img3)

                    # polygons is a list of Polygon

       for k, plgn in enumerate(plgs):
              file_output = args.output + str(k)
              print("Saving file:", file_output)

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                         210

W-2024.09
Chapter 9: Advanced Image Analysis and Metrology (AImage)
References

                        plgn.write_plx(file_output + ".plx") # export polygon as plx
                        plgn.write_text(file_output + ".txt") # export in text format
                 return

          if __name__ == "__main__":
                 main()

References

       [1] https://github.com/facebookresearch/segment-anything
       [2] Alexander Kirillov et al. "Segment anything." arXiv preprint arXiv:2304.02643 (2023).

A

ACommands

       This appendix describes the commands of the Sentaurus Calibration Workbench calibration
       modules.

Syntax Conventions

       The following conventions are used in the syntax of Python commands:
       · <a>: Array of (x,y) type, for example, ( (0.0, 1e19), (0.1, 1.3e19), (0.2, 1e18) )
       · <b>: Boolean, either True or False
       · <c>: Callable
       · <d>: Dictionary
       · <f>: Floating-point number
       · <i>: Integer
       · <k>: Keyword or type
       · <m>: Machine-learning model
       · <o>: Object
       · <p>: pandas data frame
       · <s>: String value
       · <t>: Tuple or list
       · () denotes a grouping only
       · [] denotes optional arguments
       · | denotes exclusive options

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  212

W-2024.09
Appendix A: Commands
Syntax Conventions

       Note:
            Standard Python syntax conventions are followed. Command arguments can be
            written using either <argument>=<value> or <value>. For example:

               cal.evaluate(parameters=[p1, p2, p3], targets=[t1, t2, t3],
                    parent="../parent/example")

               cal.evaluate([p1, p2, p3], [t1, t2, t3], "../parent/example")

            If only values are used, then the arguments must be written in the order presented
            in the respective command syntax.
            Only the set_option() command must be written using the format
            <argument>=<value>. This command does not accept value-only input.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                                    213

W-2024.09
Appendix A: Commands
calculate_r2

calculate_r2

       This command computes the r-squared of targets, which is the square of the Pearson
       correlation coefficient:

                                       2 C1 2 C1 2
      If show_sign=True, then r = ------------------------ .

                                              C1 1C2 2
                                         2 C1 2C1 2
      If show_sign=False, then r = --------------------- .

                                               C1 1C2 2

       In these expressions, C is the covariance matrix Cov[X,Y].

       Syntax

          calculate_r2(targets=<t>, [group_by_name=<b>,] [show_sign=<b>])

Argument   Type       Description

targets    <t>        Specifies a list of targets.

group_by_name <b>     Specifies how targets are grouped. If set to True, then the given targets
                      are grouped by their names, and r2 is calculated separately.
show_sign  <b>        Default: False

                      Specifies whether to show negative values if the values have an inverse
                      correlation. See the previous expressions. Default: False

Returns
None
Examples

 cal.calculate_r2(targets=[t1, t2, t3], group_by_name=True)

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                               214

W-2024.09
Appendix A: Commands
calibrate_ac

calibrate_ac

       This command sets the AC characteristics calibration strategy, specific to the igbtcal
       calibration module (see IGBT AC Characteristics Calibration on page 113).

       Syntax

          calibrate_ac(parameters=<t>, targets=<t>,
                                 [parent=<s>,] [child_dir=<s>,] [evaluate_only=<b>,]
                                 [tolerance_cv=<f>,] [tolerance_qg=<f>,]

calibrate_har_etch

       This command sets the calibration strategy, specific to the pmccal calibration module,
       based on a gradient-based local calibration algorithm for Sentaurus Topography high aspect
       ratio etching (see HARC Etching Calibration on page 117).

       Syntax

          calibrate_har_etch(parameters=<t>, targets=<t>,
                                            [parent=<s>,] [child_dir=<s>,]
                                            [tolerance_end=<f>,] [tolerance_mid=<f>,]
                                            [tolerance_init=<f>,] [max_iteration_end=<i>,]
                                            [max_iteration_mid=<i>,] [max_iteration_init=<i>,]
                                            [weight_factor=<f>,] [reevaluate=<b>,]
                                            [use_bi_sensitivity=<b>])

Argument              Type  Description
parameters            <t>   Specifies a list of calibration parameter objects.
targets               <t>   Specifies a list of calibration target objects.
parent                <s>   Specifies the global parent project. If set to None, then
child_dir             <s>   option("parent") is used.
tolerance_end         <f>   Specifies the directory of the child projects. If set to None, then
tolerance_mid         <f>   option("child_dir") is used.
tolerance_init        <f>   Sets the average relative error tolerance for the final calibration.
max_iteration_end     <i>   Default: 0.1
max_iteration_mid     <i>   Sets the average relative error tolerance for the middle
                            calibration. Default: 0.14
max_iteration_init    <i>   Sets the average relative error tolerance for the initial calibration.
weight_factor         <f>   Default: 0.05
                            Sets the maximum number of final calibration loops. Default: 4
                            Sets the maximum number of middle calibration loops.
                            Default: 2
                            Sets the maximum number of initial calibration loops. Default: 5
                            Specifies the factor by which the predefined weights of the
                            BottomZ and RemainingHM targets are multiplied. Default: 2.0

       This command sets the calibration strategy, specific to the pmccal calibration module,
       based on machine learning for Sentaurus Topography high aspect ratio etching (see HARC
       Etching Calibration on page 117).

       Syntax

          calibrate_har_etch_ml(parameters=<t>, targets=<t>,
                                                  [parent=<s>,] [child_dir=<s>,]

                                                  [explore_doe_method=<k>,]
                                                  [explore_doe_size=<i>,]
                                                  [tolerance_refine=<f>,]
                                                  [strategy=<s>,]
                                                  [validate_range=<b>,]
                                                  [sensitivity=<b|s|d>,]
                                                  [constraint=<s>,]
                                                  [weight_factor=<f>,]
                                                  [priority_targets=<t>,]
                                                  [num_retries=<i>,]
                                                  [num_evaluations=<i>)]

Argument               Type  Description
parameters             <t>   Specifies a list of calibration parameter objects.

parent                 <s>   Specifies the global parent project. If set to None, then
                             option("parent") is used.
child_dir              <s>   Specifies the directory of the child projects. If set to None,
                             then option("child_dir") is used.
task_name              <s>   Sets the name of the machine-learning task.
                             Default: "PMC_HAR"
tolerance              <f>   Sets the total relative RMS error tolerance. Default: 0.12
num_iterations         <i>   Sets the maximum number of iterations. Default: 10 (based
                             on the pmccal project)

                              learning. If num_parameters=0, then all defined
num_explore_parameters <i>    parameters are assumed to be primary parameters.

num_refine_parameters <i>     Sets the number of primary parameters used for

                              name is provided, then it sets the name of a CSV file that
strategy               <s>    specifies the parameter ranges for the DoE. Default: None
                              Sets the DoE method. Options are "box-behnken",
validate_range         <b>    "full-factorial", "gaussian", "latin-hypercube",
                              "uniform", and "sobol".

                              "uniform", and "sobol". Default: None
                              Sets the tolerance value for the machine-learning
                              refinement algorithm. Default: tolerance + 0.03
                              Specifies the strategy. For good initial parameter values
                              and ranges, use "standard". If the initial parameters are
                              far from optimal or the nominal simulation failed, then use
                              "validate". Default: "validate"
                              Specifies whether to perform a parameter range validation

calibrate_ml

calibrate_ml

       This command sets the calibration strategy based on machine learning.

       Syntax

          calibrate_ml(parameters=<t>, targets=<t>,
                                 [parent=<s>,] [child_dir=<s>,]
                                 [task_name=<s>,] [tolerance=<f>,]
                                 [num_iterations=<i>,]
                                 [num_validations=<i>,]
                                 [num_parameters=<i>,]
                                 [num_explore_parameters=<i>,]
                                 [num_refine_parameters=<i>,]
                                 [explore_doe_range=<f>,]
                                 [explore_doe_method=<k>,]
                                 [explore_doe_size=<i>,]
                                 [tolerance_refine=<f>,]
                                 [strategy=<s>,]

parent                <s>   Specifies the parent project. If set to None, then
                            option("parent") is used.

                            then option("child_dir") is used.

Appendix A: Commands
calibrate_ml

Argument              Type     Description

num_parameters        <i>      Sets the number of primary parameters used for machine

                               learning. If num_parameters=0, then all defined
                               parameters are assumed to be primary parameters.
                               Default: 0

num_explore_parameters <i>     Sets the number of primary parameters used for
                               exploration. If num_explore_parameters=0, then all
                               defined parameters are assumed to be primary
                               parameters. Default: None

                               refinement. If num_refine_parameters=0, then all
                               defined parameters are assumed to be primary
                               parameters. Default: None

                               specifies the parameter ranges for the DoE. Default: None

                               "uniform", and "sobol". Default: None

                               refinement algorithm. Default: tolerance + 0.03

strategy              <s>      Specifies the strategy. For good initial parameter values

                               and ranges, specify "standard". If the initial parameters
                               are far from optimal or the nominal simulation failed, then
                               specify "validate". Default: "validate"

validate_range        <b>      Specifies whether to perform a parameter range validation

                               analysis. Default: True

sensitivity           <b|s|d>  Specifies the type of sensitivity analysis to be performed:

                               · If True, then a sensitivity analysis is performed.

                               · If a task name is provided, then the argument specifies
                                  the existing task to be used for sensitivity.

                               · If a pandas data frame is provided, then the argument
                                  specifies the sensitivity information to be used.

                               Default: None

create_ml

       This command creates one or more machine-learning models. Data for training is generated
       or loaded from a file. Models are not trained. This command is specific to the mlcal
       calibration module (see Machine Learning Calibration Module: mlcal on page 52).

       Syntax

          create_ml(models=<t>, parameters=<t>, targets=<t>,
                            [parent=<s>,] [child_dir=<s>,]
                            [task_name=<s>,] [train_data=<s|p|t>,]
                            [merge_table=<d>,] [save_report=<b>,] [data_generation=<d>])
                            [doe_method=<k>, [doe_size=<i|t|d>]]

Argument              Type  Description
models                <t>   Specifies a list of machine-learning model objects.
parameters            <t>   Specifies a list of machine-learning model parameter objects.
targets               <t>   Specifies a list of machine-learning model target objects.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide                                               233

W-2024.09
Appendix A: Commands
create_ml

Argument              Type     Description
parent                <s>
child_dir             <s>      Specifies the global parent project. If set to None, then
task_name             <s>      option("parent") is used.
train_data            <s|p|t>
                               Specifies the directory of the child projects. If set to None, then
merge_table           <d>      option("child_dir") is used.
save_report           <b>
doe_method            <k>      Sets the name of the machine-learning task. Default: "ML_create"
doe_size              <i|t|d>
                               Specifies a file or pandas data frame containing training data. Default:
                               None
                               Specifies a file or pandas data frame or a list of them. In the case of a
                               list of more than one training data set, all data sets are merged into
                               one data set, with doe_size being determined as the total size. In this
                               case of a multi-window DoE, each training data set can be generated
                               by a different doe_method with a different doe_size, parameter
                               ranges. Default: None

                               Specifies the rules to merge multiple parameter or target names into
                               a single parameter or target, so that one simulation can be split into
                               different data entries. Default: None

                               Specifies whether to save a report in the report folder. Default: True

                               Sets the DoE method. Options are "adaptive", "box-behnken",
                               "full-factorial", "gaussian", "grid", "latin-hypercube",
                               "uniform", and "sobol".

                               Sets the number of samples for the DoE in the case of the DoE
                               methods "latin-hypercube", "uniform", and "sobol".
                               Sets the number of samples per parameter in the case of the DoE
                               method "grid", which is either the same for all in the case of an integer
                               or per parameter in the case of a list or dictionary.

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  234

W-2024.09
Appendix A: Commands
create_ml

Argument              Type  Description

data_generation <d>         Specifies data generation information (default: None). Dictionary keys
                            are:

                            · doe_method=<k>: Sets the DoE method. Options are "adaptive",
                               "box-behnken", "full-factorial", "gaussian", "grid",
                               "latin-hypercube", "uniform", and "sobol".

                            · doe_size=<i|t|d>: Sets the number of samples for the DoE in the
                               case of the DoE methods "latin-hypercube", "uniform", and
                               "sobol".
                               Sets the number of samples per parameter in the case of the DoE
                               method "grid", which is either the same for all in the case of an
                               integer or per parameter in the case of a list or dictionary.

                            · random_seed=<i>: Sets the random seed for the DoE in the case
                               of the DoE methods "latin-hypercube", "uniform", and
                               "sobol".

                            · use_mean=<b>: Specifies whether the nominal value is the mean of
                               the minimum and maximum values. If False, then the nominal
                               value is the user-specified value. Default: True

                            · include_nominal=<b>: If True, then the nominal value is
                               included. Default: False

                            · batch_size=<i>: Used only for adaptive generation. Sets the
                               number of data points generated before each update on the
                               parameter filter. Default: 1

                            · pass_rate=<f>: Used only for adaptive generation. Defines the
                               required passing rate of the filter. Default: 1.0

                            · threshold_num_pass=<i>: Used only for adaptive generation.
                               Sets the threshold number of passing cases to invoke the
                               parameter filter, before which the filter is all-passing. Default: 1

                            · initial_sampling_doe=<s>: Used only for adaptive generation.
                               The initial few batches can use a different DoE method other than
                               "uniform". Default: "uniform"

                            · initial_doe_batches=<i>: Used only for adaptive generation.
                               Specifies how many batches can use the different DoE method,
                               before switching to uniform randomization. If it is negative, then
                               initial_sampling_doe is used throughout the data generation
                               session. Default: -1

                            · transformer=<m>: Used only for adaptive generation. Sets the
                               transformer model to use. Default: None

                            · classifier=<m>: Used only for adaptive generation. Sets the
                               classifier model to use. Default: None

                            · unadapted_size=<i>: Sets the number of unadapted data points
                               (batch size) generated before each update on the parameter filter,
                               where 0 <= unadapted_size < batch_size. Default: 0

SentaurusTM Calibration Workbench User Guide  235
